- **P-DEC25.** *Carried (DEC-A checker N2).* The folder rule of a course's pages is written in three places: `build.py` (`md_courses` pid and `split()` `data-ctx`), `verify.py` `sub_pages` and `private_terms.folders()`, which keys on the course's `tracks` keys while the build keys on the pack's sub-course keys; equal today, they can drift. Make one shared helper. ⚠ None (kit only; baseline must stay clean)

**2. Stage the writer's packet** in one folder outside the repo with `python3 kit/thin.py stage writer --row W-SCE10 <folder>`. It holds: `brief.txt`, built by script with `open_brief` (the preamble, the Aim, Takes and Gives lines, the unit brief; never reworded, never retyped); `skills/write-unit/SKILL.md`; `docs/sourcing-standard.md` Parts 1 and 8; the figure kinds; `courses/supply-chain-execution/notes/case-facts.md`; `courses/supply-chain-execution/notes/bibliography.md`; every unit of this course already filed: none yet; `flags.txt`. The chat opens none of it.

Open flags that name this unit:
none

**This row in particular.** Search off. Claim C9 belongs here and in SCE16: attribute each side of the one-vendor argument to the kind of party that makes it, naming no vendor.

**3. The writer.** One fresh Agent on model opus, isolated, that sees only its folder. It states its model in its first line; if that is not claude-opus-5-5, stop at once, change nothing and say so in one line. Tell it:
- A memory snapshot, project instructions or anything of that kind in its context is not a source and must not be used. It uses no memory, past-chat, Projects, connector, Artifact or Agent tool and no `mcp__` tool. It publishes nothing.
- The web rule: WebSearch first; WebFetch only a URL that a search in the same agent returned verbatim, never one typed, printed in a file, edited or reached by a redirect; a refused, redirected or timed-out fetch is "not checked" and is never retried or fetched from a copy.
- It follows `skills/write-unit` §4 to §5, renders every figure before handing in, and writes its files as it goes, so a restart loses little.
- The writer rules of this course (`docs/supply-chain-execution-prompts.md` §1). The writer is one fresh Opus 5.5 agent with its packet and nothing else.
1. **Open before citing.** Every reference is found by a search and read at its publisher's page, its DOI address or the issuing body's own page before it carries a claim. The seed bibliography says only that an entry exists.
2. **Source log.** `out/sources.md`: one row per reference with the address opened, what was read (abstract, reprint or full text), the date, and the claims it carries. A reference that could not be opened is listed as not opened and carries nothing. A title is the article's own title, never a slug; a figure is cited to the paper that reports it, not to one that repeats it; each Sources entry is checked against the publisher's record before hand-in.
3. **Fewer, better.** Prefer one source read in full to three read in abstract. Do not cite to decorate. If a section's support is one textbook's account, grade it practice and say so.
4. **Grade every claim** by SCE1's key. From SCE2 on, the key arrives in the packet inside SCE1.
5. **Vendors.** Assess, rank, compare or recommend no real vendor, and map no real vendor's product line. Name a real vendor only to attribute a dated public statement to it, graded vendor-reported, or as an entry in a dated list from a named analyst report or an audited filing, every vendor on the list alike. Worked product examples use Halvern Systems, Varnby Software and Kestrow, from the case register, labelled fictional. Never say or hint which real company a fictional one resembles.
6. **Analyst reports.** Say how each was read. If only the abstract could be opened, the unit states only what the abstract states.
7. **No private figures.** No price, revenue, margin or win rate of a private company unless it published it. Where a brief asks for a cost line with no public figure, write "no public figure".
8. **The case** comes from `case-facts.md` only; invented facts go in the notes.
9. **Scope.** No AI features. No interview framing. No craft method taught. Nothing about the Project's owner, his employer, his role or his work.
10. **The two closing sections** before the deliverable: "On a site visit" and "Self-check", every answer drawn from the unit's sourced text.
11. **Flags and corrections.** A contradiction with a filed unit or a brief, a mistake noticed in a filed unit, or a need the syllabus does not meet, is written to `out/notes.md` (a contradiction or a mistake under **Contradictions**, a mistake with the quoted passage, what is wrong, the source and the exact replacement; a need under **Needs**), naming every unit it affects and with a proposed change, so that the filer writes it into `syllabus-flags.md` in that file's form. It is carried by the carry-forward rule above. The writer never rewords a brief.
12. **Hand-in.** The unit, `out/unit.md` (front matter, the unit, the deliverable, Sources); `out/sources.md`; `out/notes.md` with three lists, each item naming the unit codes it touches, an empty list written as "none": **Case facts** (rule 8), **Contradictions** and **Needs** (rule 11); the figures rendered; `out/unsure.md`, up to ten claims it is unsure of, closing with the `Object:` line; a reply of six lines at most (its model, the files it wrote, the counts of its lists, the number of references opened and not opened). `out/stats.json` is not the writer's: the chat writes it from these files with `python3 kit/thin.py stats`.

**4. The fresh check.** A separate fresh Agent on model opus that has not seen the writing, under the same agent rules, **with live search**: the prompt of `skills/audit-units` §3 with its figures paragraph, on a packet made with `python3 kit/thin.py stage checker --row W-SCE10 <folder> --unit out/unit.md --notes out/notes.md --unsure out/unsure.md --sources out/sources.md`. It writes `out/report.md` (or returns it as its reply, which the chat saves verbatim, the agent's line `Verdict: SCE10 · keep|fix|rewrite · counts` ahead of it) and replies in at most six lines. Beyond the skill's items it runs the seven passes of `docs/supply-chain-execution-prompts.md` §2 and reports each by name. One fresh Opus 5.5 agent that has not seen the unit written. It writes findings (error, should-fix, minor) and edits nothing. A reference that does not exist stops the row.
1. **References exist and match.** Every Sources entry against its publisher's or Crossref's record: authors, title, journal, year, volume, pages, DOI.
2. **Claims against sources.** Every cited claim opened and read against its source; "unverifiable" where the source will not open, never a pass.
3. **Grades.** Each grade against SCE1's key; a vendor's statement never carries more than vendor-reported; an abstract-only reading is said to be one.
4. **Vendors.** No real vendor assessed, ranked, compared, recommended or mapped; each real vendor named only as writer rule 5 allows; every list complete and alike; no private company's unpublished figure; no fictional vendor tied to a real one.
5. **The case.** Every case fact against `case-facts.md`; labelled fictional; never used as evidence.
6. **Scope and tone.** No AI features, no interview framing, no method teaching, nothing about the Project's owner or his employer; the preamble's tone rules; terms defined on first use.
7. **Deliverable and figures.** The deliverable against the brief's wording, built in full and then as a template; "On a site visit" and "Self-check" present and agreeing with the body; every figure's labels and numbers traced to the text.
It also runs the unit against Part 8 line by line and reads it as the reader in the preamble would. Before any fix, `python3 kit/thin.py save-report --row W-SCE10 --report out/report.md --model <the model it stated> --chat <this session's link> --date <today>` saves the report as `audits/supply-chain-execution/fresh-check-SCE10.md`; `python3 kit/thin.py assemble out/unit.md out/unsure.md "<the line it prints>"` gives the unit its unsure list. A `rewrite` verdict sends the unit to a new writer from the same brief, once; a second stops the row with a note.

**5. Apply.** A fresh applier agent (staged with `thin.py stage applier --row W-SCE10 <folder> --unit <unit> --report <report>`) verifies each finding against the unit and the cited source and writes `out/apply.json` (`apply` or `hold`, with the reason); `python3 kit/thin.py apply <unit> fixes.json out/apply.json` applies it as exact replacements, the unit left unchanged if one fails, the applier re-run on the failures once. Every `hold` is recorded with its reason; a held error or should-fix is a flag. A figure that fails is fixed and re-checked by a new agent, as many times as it takes; figures are never dropped. Restarts: when the harness says it is resuming an agent, let it and say so in the record; if an agent is lost, re-run it from the start once; if lost again, start a fresh agent on the same packet that resumes from the lost run's working files. Look for agent transcripts (`.jsonl` under `/root/.claude/` or `/tmp/`); where they exist, check by script that each agent used no tool it was denied and read nothing outside its folder, and take its `"model"` field; where there are none, say so, and the model is the one the agent stated.

**6. Mistakes in filed units of this course** are corrected where they sit, at step 7b. The course has no source units.

**7. Syllabus flags.** Append every Contradiction and Need to `courses/supply-chain-execution/notes/syllabus-flags.md` (next number, date, raised by SCE10, the units affected, the flag, the proposed change, status open); set to `carried` each flag this unit was given and dealt with. The writer's Case facts reach the table "Added by units" of `case-facts.md` through the filer at step 8 (`case_lines` in `records.json`, one row each, inserted by `thin.py insert`); the chat does not open the notes. Change no brief: `briefs.md` and the units in `course.toml` are edited only by a syllabus review that Pedro confirms. If a flag means the next unit cannot be written as briefed, say so in the first line of the reply and write a syllabus-review prompt in place of the next unit's.

**7b. Proposals now** (`docs/public-courses.md` §3 rule 12): every correction to a filed unit or a record, and every kit fix the work needs, is made in this chat before the build, under that rule's limits, each change to a unit checked by a fresh Agent on model opus first. Only a brief change and a task for Pedro stay *proposed*.

**8. File** per `skills/file-unit`, by a fresh filer agent and a script: front matter (model: the model the writer stated) on `courses/supply-chain-execution/SCE10-<slug>.md`; the filer, staged with `python3 kit/thin.py stage filer --row W-SCE10 <folder> --unit <unit> --notes <notes> --unsure <unsure> --report <report>`, writes `out/records.json` (the reading-page intro, saying what the unit is, how it was made and its known gaps, among them every reference not opened and every abstract-only claim; `STATUS`, `NEXT_UNIT`, `VERSION`, the hub `CHANGELOG` line, the case facts as `case_lines`, the flag rows, the `docs/courses-index.md` edits with a staleness row if search is on, both changelog entries, the `Object:` line); `python3 kit/thin.py insert out/records.json` inserts it and restores everything if it fails. Then `python3 kit/index_check.py` until it matches and `python3 kit/private_terms.py` prints OK.

**9. Build and verify.** `cd kit && python3 build.py` (read its last line), `python3 verify.py` (ALL CHECKS PASSED), then `python3 verify.py --shots <dir> --page read-sce10` (check the id it prints) in the background with a one-hour timeout; look at the page's shots at phone and desktop, dark and light, and at every figure's frames. A layout fault is fixed in `design/theme.css` (then `cp design/theme.css kit/shell.css`) or `kit/viz.py`, never in a page.

**10. Publish Academy once.** Before the build you publish, move the row to `docs/CHANGELOG.md`, move its entry in `docs/site-log.md` from Next to Done and save the next prompt in its row (step 11), so the published Backlog and Changelog pages are current. **Land before you publish** (`docs/method.md` §10): commit everything above, then `python3 kit/land.py land <this row> --expected <file>`; publish only after it exits 0. Right before the publish, `git fetch` and require `git rev-parse HEAD` to equal `git rev-parse origin/main` (if main has moved, `git pull --ff-only` first), rebuild that HEAD (`cd kit && python3 build.py`) and publish that build, never one built before landing. Read the artifact https://claude.ai/artifact/WwmQpUSqAUJvrcmLczppEH first and compare the live page with the build you started from (`get_artifact` for the live version id; `list_files` for every file's sha256 against `sha256sum` of the start build's files, the `kit/academy/` of the commit you cloned, saved outside the repo before any build). Send only the files that differ from the live artifact's (their sha256 in `list_files`), found by script, and the shell inside the wrapper the live page carries (its first line and its closing `</body></html>`, taken from the saved live shell by script into a copy outside the repo), with `slug`, `base_version` and mode patch; read the sent files back and compare their sha256 with the build; never a new artifact, never forced. Then `./deploy.sh` once: attach `apcanario/academy` with add_repo (if refused, carry on and ask Pedro once at the end), clone it once, set `ACADEMY_DIR` to the clone, and confirm with `gh run` that the deployment succeeded. The publish and deploy identifiers go into the records after the publish, committed and landed again with `kit/land.py`, without republishing.

**11. Close** per `docs/method.md` §8, steps 1 to 5: the row moved to `docs/CHANGELOG.md` with the date, the models and a one-line result; its entry in `docs/site-log.md` moved from Next to Done ("SCE10, The seams, written"; "course SCE complete" on the last unit); corrections left open go to the Corrections section, proposals to Carried proposals with their ⚠ flags; the Project copies `claude/BACKLOG-chats.md` and `claude/CHANGELOG-chats.md` rewritten, and the course's three data mirrors (`claude/supply-chain-execution-course.toml`, `claude/supply-chain-execution-briefs.md`, `claude/supply-chain-execution-syllabus-flags.md`) refreshed when this row changed them, but only once they exist: S-SCE0 did not add them while sport-psychology's were in the Project (`docs/method.md` §8 step 4); the Project holds the data mirrors only of the course being written, and the row that files that course's last unit deletes them, so this course's are added by the row that files the last unit of whichever course is being written when they are due; a row of this course that finds them absent after that adds all three from their repo files (`project_write` with `local_path`); while sport-psychology's are still in the Project, none is written; `python3 kit/next_prompt.py --save` saves the prompt of W-SCE11 (unit SCE11).

**Reply:** what was filed where (words, figures, references opened and not opened); the fresh-check verdict and the result of each of the seven passes; each mistake corrected, in a line; each flag raised, with the units it affects and whether it blocks the next unit; the private-terms result; verify's result; the publish and the deploy; the choices taken; the backlog hand-off; every proposal listed with its ⚠ flag, and every task still open for Pedro by hand (the rows under Proposed marked for him: P-SCE1 and P-SCE6), repeated in every reply until he marks it done; then the next prompt in a code block. |

Right after cloning, run `python3 kit/land.py start <this row>` (it records the start commit and installs the push guard). Row W-SCE11 of `docs/BACKLOG.md`, in this chat: write, fresh-check and file unit SCE11, "The buying committee", of the public course Supply Chain Execution for Product Teams (`courses/supply-chain-execution/course.toml`: data `courses/supply-chain-execution/notes/`, vault `courses/supply-chain-execution/`, `docs/sourcing-standard.md` Parts 1 and 8; search on); correct every mistake the work finds; flag every contradiction or need that touches another unit; then build, verify, land (`kit/land.py land`), then publish Academy once from a fresh build of the landed HEAD (the artifact, then GitHub Pages), close the row and end with the next row's prompt.

Read first: `docs/public-courses.md`, `docs/method.md` §4, §5, §7 and §8, `skills/write-unit`, `skills/audit-units` §3, `skills/file-unit` and the docstring of `kit/thin.py` (the chat reads those, never a unit).

**This is a thin main chat** (method §4). (1) The chat runs on Sonnet 5.5 and the record says which model it ran as. The writer, the fresh checker, the applier, the filer and the corrections checker are each a fresh Agent on model opus, isolated on a packet. (2) The chat never opens a unit, a report or a brief. It stages each packet with `python3 kit/thin.py stage <writer|checker|applier|filer> --row W-SCE11 <folder>`, launches the agent, and reads only the agent's reply, at most six lines, and the `out/stats.json` that `python3 kit/thin.py stats <out folder>` writes. Every agent writes its full output to files in its `out/` folder. (3) Scripts, not the chat, check the result: `kit/index_check.py`, `kit/private_terms.py`, the last line of `build.py`, `verify.py`, the publish read-back by sha256. (4) A checkpoint commit follows every agent's return, on `main`; it is landed at step 10 with `kit/land.py` (never a bare push). (5) No unit is uploaded to the Project. If the session's permission check refuses to run the repo's own scripts, stop, say so in one line and ask Pedro; never work around it. Where a choice is open, take the most reasonable reading, say so in the record and carry on; ask only if blocked. Fix every error, should-fix and minor finding the work turns up in this chat.

**The carry-forward rule (Pedro, standing; `docs/supply-chain-execution-prompts.md` §4, `docs/method.md` §9).** Nothing found goes unrecorded or waits for a later audit. It applies to every row of this course, the last included.

1. **What counts.** Any error, mistake, contradiction, unverified claim, missing source or unmet need, found before, during or after a unit is written: by the writer, the fresh checker, the applier, the filer, the corrections checker, a script, or Pedro.
2. **About a unit already filed.** It goes into the next row's prompt as a correction, with the unit, the passage, what is wrong and the source. The next chat fixes it first, before it writes its own unit: each change is checked by a fresh agent against its source and the syllabus, applied as an exact replacement, and recorded.
3. **About a unit not yet written.** It becomes a flag in `syllabus-flags.md` naming every unit it affects, and it is printed in each of those units' prompts as a guideline for the writer. A flag that would change a brief is proposed, never applied by the chat that raised it.
4. **About the unit being written.** It is fixed in the same chat before filing. What cannot be fixed (a source that will not open) is cut or marked unverified in the unit, and carried as in 2.
5. **Every delivery ends with the next prompt.** The reply of every row closes with the prompt for the next unit in a code block, as `kit/next_prompt.py` prints it, already holding: every correction still open on a filed unit, every open flag that names the next unit, and any guideline of this prompt that the row showed needs updating, with the update made. A row that finds a rule of the template wrong or unworkable corrects it in `docs/supply-chain-execution-prompts.md`, says what it changed, and the next prompt carries the corrected text.
6. **Nothing is dropped silently.** A correction or flag leaves the next prompt only when it is resolved, with the row and the report that resolved it written beside it.
7. **The last row** (W-DD-SCE2) delivers a closing corrections prompt in place of a next unit's, listing every correction and flag still open.

11. **Flags and corrections.** A contradiction with a filed unit or a brief, a mistake noticed in a filed unit, or a need the syllabus does not meet, is written to `out/notes.md` (a contradiction or a mistake under **Contradictions**, a mistake with the quoted passage, what is wrong, the source and the exact replacement; a need under **Needs**), naming every unit it affects and with a proposed change, so that the filer writes it into `syllabus-flags.md` in that file's form. It is carried by the carry-forward rule above. The writer never rewords a brief.
12. **Hand-in.** The unit, `out/unit.md` (front matter, the unit, the deliverable, Sources); `out/sources.md`; `out/notes.md` with three lists, each item naming the unit codes it touches, an empty list written as "none": **Case facts** (rule 8), **Contradictions** and **Needs** (rule 11); the figures rendered; `out/unsure.md`, up to ten claims it is unsure of, closing with the `Object:` line; a reply of six lines at most (its model, the files it wrote, the counts of its lists, the number of references opened and not opened). `out/stats.json` is not the writer's: the chat writes it from these files with `python3 kit/thin.py stats`.

**4. The fresh check.** A separate fresh Agent on model opus that has not seen the writing, under the same agent rules, **with live search**: the prompt of `skills/audit-units` §3 with its figures paragraph, on a packet made with `python3 kit/thin.py stage checker --row W-SCE11 <folder> --unit out/unit.md --notes out/notes.md --unsure out/unsure.md --sources out/sources.md`. It writes `out/report.md` (or returns it as its reply, which the chat saves verbatim, the agent's line `Verdict: SCE11 · keep|fix|rewrite · counts` ahead of it) and replies in at most six lines. Beyond the skill's items it runs the seven passes of `docs/supply-chain-execution-prompts.md` §2 and reports each by name. One fresh Opus 5.5 agent that has not seen the unit written. It writes findings (error, should-fix, minor) and edits nothing. A reference that does not exist stops the row.
1. **References exist and match.** Every Sources entry against its publisher's or Crossref's record: authors, title, journal, year, volume, pages, DOI.
2. **Claims against sources.** Every cited claim opened and read against its source; "unverifiable" where the source will not open, never a pass.
3. **Grades.** Each grade against SCE1's key; a vendor's statement never carries more than vendor-reported; an abstract-only reading is said to be one.
4. **Vendors.** No real vendor assessed, ranked, compared, recommended or mapped; each real vendor named only as writer rule 5 allows; every list complete and alike; no private company's unpublished figure; no fictional vendor tied to a real one.
5. **The case.** Every case fact against `case-facts.md`; labelled fictional; never used as evidence.
6. **Scope and tone.** No AI features, no interview framing, no method teaching, nothing about the Project's owner or his employer; the preamble's tone rules; terms defined on first use.
7. **Deliverable and figures.** The deliverable against the brief's wording, built in full and then as a template; "On a site visit" and "Self-check" present and agreeing with the body; every figure's labels and numbers traced to the text.
It also runs the unit against Part 8 line by line and reads it as the reader in the preamble would. Before any fix, `python3 kit/thin.py save-report --row W-SCE11 --report out/report.md --model <the model it stated> --chat <this session's link> --date <today>` saves the report as `audits/supply-chain-execution/fresh-check-SCE11.md`; `python3 kit/thin.py assemble out/unit.md out/unsure.md "<the line it prints>"` gives the unit its unsure list. A `rewrite` verdict sends the unit to a new writer from the same brief, once; a second stops the row with a note.

**5. Apply.** A fresh applier agent (staged with `thin.py stage applier --row W-SCE11 <folder> --unit <unit> --report <report>`) verifies each finding against the unit and the cited source and writes `out/apply.json` (`apply` or `hold`, with the reason); `python3 kit/thin.py apply <unit> fixes.json out/apply.json` applies it as exact replacements, the unit left unchanged if one fails, the applier re-run on the failures once. Every `hold` is recorded with its reason; a held error or should-fix is a flag. A figure that fails is fixed and re-checked by a new agent, as many times as it takes; figures are never dropped. Restarts: when the harness says it is resuming an agent, let it and say so in the record; if an agent is lost, re-run it from the start once; if lost again, start a fresh agent on the same packet that resumes from the lost run's working files. Look for agent transcripts (`.jsonl` under `/root/.claude/` or `/tmp/`); where they exist, check by script that each agent used no tool it was denied and read nothing outside its folder, and take its `"model"` field; where there are none, say so, and the model is the one the agent stated.

**6. Mistakes in filed units of this course** are corrected where they sit, at step 7b. The course has no source units.

**7. Syllabus flags.** Append every Contradiction and Need to `courses/supply-chain-execution/notes/syllabus-flags.md` (next number, date, raised by SCE11, the units affected, the flag, the proposed change, status open); set to `carried` each flag this unit was given and dealt with. The writer's Case facts reach the table "Added by units" of `case-facts.md` through the filer at step 8 (`case_lines` in `records.json`, one row each, inserted by `thin.py insert`); the chat does not open the notes. Change no brief: `briefs.md` and the units in `course.toml` are edited only by a syllabus review that Pedro confirms. If a flag means the next unit cannot be written as briefed, say so in the first line of the reply and write a syllabus-review prompt in place of the next unit's.

**7b. Proposals now** (`docs/public-courses.md` §3 rule 12): every correction to a filed unit or a record, and every kit fix the work needs, is made in this chat before the build, under that rule's limits, each change to a unit checked by a fresh Agent on model opus first. Only a brief change and a task for Pedro stay *proposed*.

**8. File** per `skills/file-unit`, by a fresh filer agent and a script: front matter (model: the model the writer stated) on `courses/supply-chain-execution/SCE11-<slug>.md`; the filer, staged with `python3 kit/thin.py stage filer --row W-SCE11 <folder> --unit <unit> --notes <notes> --unsure <unsure> --report <report>`, writes `out/records.json` (the reading-page intro, saying what the unit is, how it was made and its known gaps, among them every reference not opened and every abstract-only claim; `STATUS`, `NEXT_UNIT`, `VERSION`, the hub `CHANGELOG` line, the case facts as `case_lines`, the flag rows, the `docs/courses-index.md` edits with a staleness row if search is on, both changelog entries, the `Object:` line); `python3 kit/thin.py insert out/records.json` inserts it and restores everything if it fails. Then `python3 kit/index_check.py` until it matches and `python3 kit/private_terms.py` prints OK.

**9. Build and verify.** `cd kit && python3 build.py` (read its last line), `python3 verify.py` (ALL CHECKS PASSED), then `python3 verify.py --shots <dir> --page read-sce11` (check the id it prints) in the background with a one-hour timeout; look at the page's shots at phone and desktop, dark and light, and at every figure's frames. A layout fault is fixed in `design/theme.css` (then `cp design/theme.css kit/shell.css`) or `kit/viz.py`, never in a page.

**10. Publish Academy once.** Before the build you publish, move the row to `docs/CHANGELOG.md`, move its entry in `docs/site-log.md` from Next to Done and save the next prompt in its row (step 11), so the published Backlog and Changelog pages are current. **Land before you publish** (`docs/method.md` §10): commit everything above, then `python3 kit/land.py land <this row> --expected <file>`; publish only after it exits 0. Right before the publish, `git fetch` and require `git rev-parse HEAD` to equal `git rev-parse origin/main` (if main has moved, `git pull --ff-only` first), rebuild that HEAD (`cd kit && python3 build.py`) and publish that build, never one built before landing. Read the artifact https://claude.ai/artifact/WwmQpUSqAUJvrcmLczppEH first and compare the live page with the build you started from (`get_artifact` for the live version id; `list_files` for every file's sha256 against `sha256sum` of the start build's files, the `kit/academy/` of the commit you cloned, saved outside the repo before any build). Send only the files that differ from the live artifact's (their sha256 in `list_files`), found by script, and the shell inside the wrapper the live page carries (its first line and its closing `</body></html>`, taken from the saved live shell by script into a copy outside the repo), with `slug`, `base_version` and mode patch; read the sent files back and compare their sha256 with the build; never a new artifact, never forced. Then `./deploy.sh` once: attach `apcanario/academy` with add_repo (if refused, carry on and ask Pedro once at the end), clone it once, set `ACADEMY_DIR` to the clone, and confirm with `gh run` that the deployment succeeded. The publish and deploy identifiers go into the records after the publish, committed and landed again with `kit/land.py`, without republishing.

**11. Close** per `docs/method.md` §8, steps 1 to 5: the row moved to `docs/CHANGELOG.md` with the date, the models and a one-line result; its entry in `docs/site-log.md` moved from Next to Done ("SCE11, The buying committee, written"; "course SCE complete" on the last unit); corrections left open go to the Corrections section, proposals to Carried proposals with their ⚠ flags; the Project copies `claude/BACKLOG-chats.md` and `claude/CHANGELOG-chats.md` rewritten, and the course's three data mirrors (`claude/supply-chain-execution-course.toml`, `claude/supply-chain-execution-briefs.md`, `claude/supply-chain-execution-syllabus-flags.md`) refreshed when this row changed them, but only once they exist: S-SCE0 did not add them while sport-psychology's were in the Project (`docs/method.md` §8 step 4); the Project holds the data mirrors only of the course being written, and the row that files that course's last unit deletes them, so this course's are added by the row that files the last unit of whichever course is being written when they are due; a row of this course that finds them absent after that adds all three from their repo files (`project_write` with `local_path`); while sport-psychology's are still in the Project, none is written; `python3 kit/next_prompt.py --save` saves the prompt of W-SCE12 (unit SCE12).

**This is a thin main chat** (method §4). (1) The chat runs on Sonnet 5.5 and the record says which model it ran as. The writer, the fresh checker, the applier, the filer and the corrections checker are each a fresh Agent on model opus, isolated on a packet. (2) The chat never opens a unit, a report or a brief. It stages each packet with `python3 kit/thin.py stage <writer|checker|applier|filer> --row W-SCE12 <folder>`, launches the agent, and reads only the agent's reply, at most six lines, and the `out/stats.json` that `python3 kit/thin.py stats <out folder>` writes. Every agent writes its full output to files in its `out/` folder. (3) Scripts, not the chat, check the result: `kit/index_check.py`, `kit/private_terms.py`, the last line of `build.py`, `verify.py`, the publish read-back by sha256. (4) A checkpoint commit follows every agent's return, on `main`; it is landed at step 10 with `kit/land.py` (never a bare push). (5) No unit is uploaded to the Project. If the session's permission check refuses to run the repo's own scripts, stop, say so in one line and ask Pedro; never work around it. Where a choice is open, take the most reasonable reading, say so in the record and carry on; ask only if blocked. Fix every error, should-fix and minor finding the work turns up in this chat.

**The carry-forward rule (Pedro, standing; `docs/supply-chain-execution-prompts.md` §4, `docs/method.md` §9).** Nothing found goes unrecorded or waits for a later audit. It applies to every row of this course, the last included.

1. **What counts.** Any error, mistake, contradiction, unverified claim, missing source or unmet need, found before, during or after a unit is written: by the writer, the fresh checker, the applier, the filer, the corrections checker, a script, or Pedro.
2. **About a unit already filed.** It goes into the next row's prompt as a correction, with the unit, the passage, what is wrong and the source. The next chat fixes it first, before it writes its own unit: each change is checked by a fresh agent against its source and the syllabus, applied as an exact replacement, and recorded.
3. **About a unit not yet written.** It becomes a flag in `syllabus-flags.md` naming every unit it affects, and it is printed in each of those units' prompts as a guideline for the writer. A flag that would change a brief is proposed, never applied by the chat that raised it.
4. **About the unit being written.** It is fixed in the same chat before filing. What cannot be fixed (a source that will not open) is cut or marked unverified in the unit, and carried as in 2.
5. **Every delivery ends with the next prompt.** The reply of every row closes with the prompt for the next unit in a code block, as `kit/next_prompt.py` prints it, already holding: every correction still open on a filed unit, every open flag that names the next unit, and any guideline of this prompt that the row showed needs updating, with the update made. A row that finds a rule of the template wrong or unworkable corrects it in `docs/supply-chain-execution-prompts.md`, says what it changed, and the next prompt carries the corrected text.
6. **Nothing is dropped silently.** A correction or flag leaves the next prompt only when it is resolved, with the row and the report that resolved it written beside it.
7. **The last row** (W-DD-SCE2) delivers a closing corrections prompt in place of a next unit's, listing every correction and flag still open.

**3. The writer.** One fresh Agent on model opus, isolated, that sees only its folder. It states its model in its first line; if that is not claude-opus-5-5, stop at once, change nothing and say so in one line. Tell it:
- A memory snapshot, project instructions or anything of that kind in its context is not a source and must not be used. It uses no memory, past-chat, Projects, connector, Artifact or Agent tool and no `mcp__` tool. It publishes nothing.
- The web rule: WebSearch first; WebFetch only a URL that a search in the same agent returned verbatim, never one typed, printed in a file, edited or reached by a redirect; a refused, redirected or timed-out fetch is "not checked" and is never retried or fetched from a copy.
- It follows `skills/write-unit` §4 to §5, renders every figure before handing in, and writes its files as it goes, so a restart loses little.
- The writer rules of this course (`docs/supply-chain-execution-prompts.md` §1). The writer is one fresh Opus 5.5 agent with its packet and nothing else.
1. **Open before citing.** Every reference is found by a search and read at its publisher's page, its DOI address or the issuing body's own page before it carries a claim. The seed bibliography says only that an entry exists.
2. **Source log.** `out/sources.md`: one row per reference with the address opened, what was read (abstract, reprint or full text), the date, and the claims it carries. A reference that could not be opened is listed as not opened and carries nothing. A title is the article's own title, never a slug; a figure is cited to the paper that reports it, not to one that repeats it; each Sources entry is checked against the publisher's record before hand-in.
3. **Fewer, better.** Prefer one source read in full to three read in abstract. Do not cite to decorate. If a section's support is one textbook's account, grade it practice and say so.

**This is a thin main chat** (method §4). (1) The chat runs on Sonnet 5.5 and the record says which model it ran as. The writer, the fresh checker, the applier, the filer and the corrections checker are each a fresh Agent on model opus, isolated on a packet. (2) The chat never opens a unit, a report or a brief. It stages each packet with `python3 kit/thin.py stage <writer|checker|applier|filer> --row W-SCE13 <folder>`, launches the agent, and reads only the agent's reply, at most six lines, and the `out/stats.json` that `python3 kit/thin.py stats <out folder>` writes. Every agent writes its full output to files in its `out/` folder. (3) Scripts, not the chat, check the result: `kit/index_check.py`, `kit/private_terms.py`, the last line of `build.py`, `verify.py`, the publish read-back by sha256. (4) A checkpoint commit follows every agent's return, on `main`; it is landed at step 10 with `kit/land.py` (never a bare push). (5) No unit is uploaded to the Project. If the session's permission check refuses to run the repo's own scripts, stop, say so in one line and ask Pedro; never work around it. Where a choice is open, take the most reasonable reading, say so in the record and carry on; ask only if blocked. Fix every error, should-fix and minor finding the work turns up in this chat.

**The carry-forward rule (Pedro, standing; `docs/supply-chain-execution-prompts.md` §4, `docs/method.md` §9).** Nothing found goes unrecorded or waits for a later audit. It applies to every row of this course, the last included.

1. **What counts.** Any error, mistake, contradiction, unverified claim, missing source or unmet need, found before, during or after a unit is written: by the writer, the fresh checker, the applier, the filer, the corrections checker, a script, or Pedro.
2. **About a unit already filed.** It goes into the next row's prompt as a correction, with the unit, the passage, what is wrong and the source. The next chat fixes it first, before it writes its own unit: each change is checked by a fresh agent against its source and the syllabus, applied as an exact replacement, and recorded.
3. **About a unit not yet written.** It becomes a flag in `syllabus-flags.md` naming every unit it affects, and it is printed in each of those units' prompts as a guideline for the writer. A flag that would change a brief is proposed, never applied by the chat that raised it.
4. **About the unit being written.** It is fixed in the same chat before filing. What cannot be fixed (a source that will not open) is cut or marked unverified in the unit, and carried as in 2.
5. **Every delivery ends with the next prompt.** The reply of every row closes with the prompt for the next unit in a code block, as `kit/next_prompt.py` prints it, already holding: every correction still open on a filed unit, every open flag that names the next unit, and any guideline of this prompt that the row showed needs updating, with the update made. A row that finds a rule of the template wrong or unworkable corrects it in `docs/supply-chain-execution-prompts.md`, says what it changed, and the next prompt carries the corrected text.
6. **Nothing is dropped silently.** A correction or flag leaves the next prompt only when it is resolved, with the row and the report that resolved it written beside it.
7. **The last row** (W-DD-SCE2) delivers a closing corrections prompt in place of a next unit's, listing every correction and flag still open.

**This row in particular.** Search off. No new role is invented: a missing role is a flag. Claim C14 belongs here.

**3. The writer.** One fresh Agent on model opus, isolated, that sees only its folder. It states its model in its first line; if that is not claude-opus-5-5, stop at once, change nothing and say so in one line. Tell it:
- A memory snapshot, project instructions or anything of that kind in its context is not a source and must not be used. It uses no memory, past-chat, Projects, connector, Artifact or Agent tool and no `mcp__` tool. It publishes nothing.
- The web rule: WebSearch first; WebFetch only a URL that a search in the same agent returned verbatim, never one typed, printed in a file, edited or reached by a redirect; a refused, redirected or timed-out fetch is "not checked" and is never retried or fetched from a copy.
- It follows `skills/write-unit` §4 to §5, renders every figure before handing in, and writes its files as it goes, so a restart loses little.
- The writer rules of this course (`docs/supply-chain-execution-prompts.md` §1). The writer is one fresh Opus 5.5 agent with its packet and nothing else.
1. **Open before citing.** Every reference is found by a search and read at its publisher's page, its DOI address or the issuing body's own page before it carries a claim. The seed bibliography says only that an entry exists.
2. **Source log.** `out/sources.md`: one row per reference with the address opened, what was read (abstract, reprint or full text), the date, and the claims it carries. A reference that could not be opened is listed as not opened and carries nothing. A title is the article's own title, never a slug; a figure is cited to the paper that reports it, not to one that repeats it; each Sources entry is checked against the publisher's record before hand-in.
3. **Fewer, better.** Prefer one source read in full to three read in abstract. Do not cite to decorate. If a section's support is one textbook's account, grade it practice and say so.
4. **Grade every claim** by SCE1's key. From SCE2 on, the key arrives in the packet inside SCE1.
5. **Vendors.** Assess, rank, compare or recommend no real vendor, and map no real vendor's product line. Name a real vendor only to attribute a dated public statement to it, graded vendor-reported, or as an entry in a dated list from a named analyst report or an audited filing, every vendor on the list alike. Worked product examples use Halvern Systems, Varnby Software and Kestrow, from the case register, labelled fictional. Never say or hint which real company a fictional one resembles.
6. **Analyst reports.** Say how each was read. If only the abstract could be opened, the unit states only what the abstract states.
7. **No private figures.** No price, revenue, margin or win rate of a private company unless it published it. Where a brief asks for a cost line with no public figure, write "no public figure".
8. **The case** comes from `case-facts.md` only; invented facts go in the notes.
9. **Scope.** No AI features. No interview framing. No craft method taught. Nothing about the Project's owner, his employer, his role or his work.
10. **The two closing sections** before the deliverable: "On a site visit" and "Self-check", every answer drawn from the unit's sourced text.
11. **Flags and corrections.** A contradiction with a filed unit or a brief, a mistake noticed in a filed unit, or a need the syllabus does not meet, is written to `out/notes.md` (a contradiction or a mistake under **Contradictions**, a mistake with the quoted passage, what is wrong, the source and the exact replacement; a need under **Needs**), naming every unit it affects and with a proposed change, so that the filer writes it into `syllabus-flags.md` in that file's form. It is carried by the carry-forward rule above. The writer never rewords a brief.
12. **Hand-in.** The unit, `out/unit.md` (front matter, the unit, the deliverable, Sources); `out/sources.md`; `out/notes.md` with three lists, each item naming the unit codes it touches, an empty list written as "none": **Case facts** (rule 8), **Contradictions** and **Needs** (rule 11); the figures rendered; `out/unsure.md`, up to ten claims it is unsure of, closing with the `Object:` line; a reply of six lines at most (its model, the files it wrote, the counts of its lists, the number of references opened and not opened). `out/stats.json` is not the writer's: the chat writes it from these files with `python3 kit/thin.py stats`.

**4. The fresh check.** A separate fresh Agent on model opus that has not seen the writing, under the same agent rules, **with live search**: the prompt of `skills/audit-units` §3 with its figures paragraph, on a packet made with `python3 kit/thin.py stage checker --row W-SCE13 <folder> --unit out/unit.md --notes out/notes.md --unsure out/unsure.md --sources out/sources.md`. It writes `out/report.md` (or returns it as its reply, which the chat saves verbatim, the agent's line `Verdict: SCE13 · keep|fix|rewrite · counts` ahead of it) and replies in at most six lines. Beyond the skill's items it runs the seven passes of `docs/supply-chain-execution-prompts.md` §2 and reports each by name. One fresh Opus 5.5 agent that has not seen the unit written. It writes findings (error, should-fix, minor) and edits nothing. A reference that does not exist stops the row.
1. **References exist and match.** Every Sources entry against its publisher's or Crossref's record: authors, title, journal, year, volume, pages, DOI.
2. **Claims against sources.** Every cited claim opened and read against its source; "unverifiable" where the source will not open, never a pass.
3. **Grades.** Each grade against SCE1's key; a vendor's statement never carries more than vendor-reported; an abstract-only reading is said to be one.
4. **Vendors.** No real vendor assessed, ranked, compared, recommended or mapped; each real vendor named only as writer rule 5 allows; every list complete and alike; no private company's unpublished figure; no fictional vendor tied to a real one.
5. **The case.** Every case fact against `case-facts.md`; labelled fictional; never used as evidence.
6. **Scope and tone.** No AI features, no interview framing, no method teaching, nothing about the Project's owner or his employer; the preamble's tone rules; terms defined on first use.
7. **Deliverable and figures.** The deliverable against the brief's wording, built in full and then as a template; "On a site visit" and "Self-check" present and agreeing with the body; every figure's labels and numbers traced to the text.
It also runs the unit against Part 8 line by line and reads it as the reader in the preamble would. Before any fix, `python3 kit/thin.py save-report --row W-SCE13 --report out/report.md --model <the model it stated> --chat <this session's link> --date <today>` saves the report as `audits/supply-chain-execution/fresh-check-SCE13.md`; `python3 kit/thin.py assemble out/unit.md out/unsure.md "<the line it prints>"` gives the unit its unsure list. A `rewrite` verdict sends the unit to a new writer from the same brief, once; a second stops the row with a note.

**5. Apply.** A fresh applier agent (staged with `thin.py stage applier --row W-SCE13 <folder> --unit <unit> --report <report>`) verifies each finding against the unit and the cited source and writes `out/apply.json` (`apply` or `hold`, with the reason); `python3 kit/thin.py apply <unit> fixes.json out/apply.json` applies it as exact replacements, the unit left unchanged if one fails, the applier re-run on the failures once. Every `hold` is recorded with its reason; a held error or should-fix is a flag. A figure that fails is fixed and re-checked by a new agent, as many times as it takes; figures are never dropped. Restarts: when the harness says it is resuming an agent, let it and say so in the record; if an agent is lost, re-run it from the start once; if lost again, start a fresh agent on the same packet that resumes from the lost run's working files. Look for agent transcripts (`.jsonl` under `/root/.claude/` or `/tmp/`); where they exist, check by script that each agent used no tool it was denied and read nothing outside its folder, and take its `"model"` field; where there are none, say so, and the model is the one the agent stated.

**This is a thin main chat** (method §4). (1) The chat runs on Sonnet 5.5 and the record says which model it ran as. The writer, the fresh checker, the applier, the filer and the corrections checker are each a fresh Agent on model opus, isolated on a packet. (2) The chat never opens a unit, a report or a brief. It stages each packet with `python3 kit/thin.py stage <writer|checker|applier|filer> --row W-SCE14 <folder>`, launches the agent, and reads only the agent's reply, at most six lines, and the `out/stats.json` that `python3 kit/thin.py stats <out folder>` writes. Every agent writes its full output to files in its `out/` folder. (3) Scripts, not the chat, check the result: `kit/index_check.py`, `kit/private_terms.py`, the last line of `build.py`, `verify.py`, the publish read-back by sha256. (4) A checkpoint commit follows every agent's return, on `main`; it is landed at step 10 with `kit/land.py` (never a bare push). (5) No unit is uploaded to the Project. If the session's permission check refuses to run the repo's own scripts, stop, say so in one line and ask Pedro; never work around it. Where a choice is open, take the most reasonable reading, say so in the record and carry on; ask only if blocked. Fix every error, should-fix and minor finding the work turns up in this chat.

**The carry-forward rule (Pedro, standing; `docs/supply-chain-execution-prompts.md` §4, `docs/method.md` §9).** Nothing found goes unrecorded or waits for a later audit. It applies to every row of this course, the last included.

1. **What counts.** Any error, mistake, contradiction, unverified claim, missing source or unmet need, found before, during or after a unit is written: by the writer, the fresh checker, the applier, the filer, the corrections checker, a script, or Pedro.
2. **About a unit already filed.** It goes into the next row's prompt as a correction, with the unit, the passage, what is wrong and the source. The next chat fixes it first, before it writes its own unit: each change is checked by a fresh agent against its source and the syllabus, applied as an exact replacement, and recorded.
3. **About a unit not yet written.** It becomes a flag in `syllabus-flags.md` naming every unit it affects, and it is printed in each of those units' prompts as a guideline for the writer. A flag that would change a brief is proposed, never applied by the chat that raised it.
4. **About the unit being written.** It is fixed in the same chat before filing. What cannot be fixed (a source that will not open) is cut or marked unverified in the unit, and carried as in 2.
5. **Every delivery ends with the next prompt.** The reply of every row closes with the prompt for the next unit in a code block, as `kit/next_prompt.py` prints it, already holding: every correction still open on a filed unit, every open flag that names the next unit, and any guideline of this prompt that the row showed needs updating, with the update made. A row that finds a rule of the template wrong or unworkable corrects it in `docs/supply-chain-execution-prompts.md`, says what it changed, and the next prompt carries the corrected text.
6. **Nothing is dropped silently.** A correction or flag leaves the next prompt only when it is resolved, with the row and the report that resolved it written beside it.
7. **The last row** (W-DD-SCE2) delivers a closing corrections prompt in place of a next unit's, listing every correction and flag still open.

**2. Stage the writer's packet** in one folder outside the repo with `python3 kit/thin.py stage writer --row W-SCE14 <folder>`. It holds: `brief.txt`, built by script with `open_brief` (the preamble, the Aim, Takes and Gives lines, the unit brief; never reworded, never retyped); `skills/write-unit/SKILL.md`; `docs/sourcing-standard.md` Parts 1 and 8; the figure kinds; `courses/supply-chain-execution/notes/case-facts.md`; `courses/supply-chain-execution/notes/bibliography.md`; every unit of this course already filed: none yet; `flags.txt`. **This row in particular.** Search off. Claim C7 belongs here: find the origin of any failure-rate figure or say none was found.

**3. The writer.** One fresh Agent on model opus, isolated, that sees only its folder. It states its model in its first line; if that is not claude-opus-5-5, stop at once, change nothing and say so in one line. Tell it:
- A memory snapshot, project instructions or anything of that kind in its context is not a source and must not be used. It uses no memory, past-chat, Projects, connector, Artifact or Agent tool and no `mcp__` tool. It publishes nothing.
- The web rule: WebSearch first; WebFetch only a URL that a search in the same agent returned verbatim, never one typed, printed in a file, edited or reached by a redirect; a refused, redirected or timed-out fetch is "not checked" and is never retried or fetched from a copy.
- It follows `skills/write-unit` §4 to §5, renders every figure before handing in, and writes its files as it goes, so a restart loses little.
- The writer rules of this course (`docs/supply-chain-execution-prompts.md` §1). The writer is one fresh Opus 5.5 agent with its packet and nothing else.
1. **Open before citing.** Every reference is found by a search and read at its publisher's page, its DOI address or the issuing body's own page before it carries a claim. The seed bibliography says only that an entry exists.
2. **Source log.** `out/sources.md`: one row per reference with the address opened, what was read (abstract, reprint or full text), the date, and the claims it carries. A reference that could not be opened is listed as not opened and carries nothing. A title is the article's own title, never a slug; a figure is cited to the paper that reports it, not to one that repeats it; each Sources entry is checked against the publisher's record before hand-in.
3. **Fewer, better.** Prefer one source read in full to three read in abstract. Do not cite to decorate. If a section's support is one textbook's account, grade it practice and say so.
4. **Grade every claim** by SCE1's key. From SCE2 on, the key arrives in the packet inside SCE1.
5. **Vendors.** Assess, rank, compare or recommend no real vendor, and map no real vendor's product line. Name a real vendor only to attribute a dated public statement to it, graded vendor-reported, or as an entry in a dated list from a named analyst report or an audited filing, every vendor on the list alike. Worked product examples use Halvern Systems, Varnby Software and Kestrow, from the case register, labelled fictional. Never say or hint which real company a fictional one resembles.
6. **Analyst reports.** Say how each was read. If only the abstract could be opened, the unit states only what the abstract states.
7. **No private figures.** No price, revenue, margin or win rate of a private company unless it published it. Where a brief asks for a cost line with no public figure, write "no public figure".
8. **The case** comes from `case-facts.md` only; invented facts go in the notes.
9. **Scope.** No AI features. No interview framing. No craft method taught. Nothing about the Project's owner, his employer, his role or his work.
10. **The two closing sections** before the deliverable: "On a site visit" and "Self-check", every answer drawn from the unit's sourced text.
11. **Flags and corrections.** A contradiction with a filed unit or a brief, a mistake noticed in a filed unit, or a need the syllabus does not meet, is written to `out/notes.md` (a contradiction or a mistake under **Contradictions**, a mistake with the quoted passage, what is wrong, the source and the exact replacement; a need under **Needs**), naming every unit it affects and with a proposed change, so that the filer writes it into `syllabus-flags.md` in that file's form. It is carried by the carry-forward rule above. The writer never rewords a brief.
12. **Hand-in.** The unit, `out/unit.md` (front matter, the unit, the deliverable, Sources); `out/sources.md`; `out/notes.md` with three lists, each item naming the unit codes it touches, an empty list written as "none": **Case facts** (rule 8), **Contradictions** and **Needs** (rule 11); the figures rendered; `out/unsure.md`, up to ten claims it is unsure of, closing with the `Object:` line; a reply of six lines at most (its model, the files it wrote, the counts of its lists, the number of references opened and not opened). `out/stats.json` is not the writer's: the chat writes it from these files with `python3 kit/thin.py stats`.

**4. The fresh check.** A separate fresh Agent on model opus that has not seen the writing, under the same agent rules, **with live search**: the prompt of `skills/audit-units` §3 with its figures paragraph, on a packet made with `python3 kit/thin.py stage checker --row W-SCE14 <folder> --unit out/unit.md --notes out/notes.md --unsure out/unsure.md --sources out/sources.md`. It writes `out/report.md` (or returns it as its reply, which the chat saves verbatim, the agent's line `Verdict: SCE14 · keep|fix|rewrite · counts` ahead of it) and replies in at most six lines. Beyond the skill's items it runs the seven passes of `docs/supply-chain-execution-prompts.md` §2 and reports each by name. One fresh Opus 5.5 agent that has not seen the unit written. It writes findings (error, should-fix, minor) and edits nothing. A reference that does not exist stops the row.
1. **References exist and match.** Every Sources entry against its publisher's or Crossref's record: authors, title, journal, year, volume, pages, DOI.
2. **Claims against sources.** Every cited claim opened and read against its source; "unverifiable" where the source will not open, never a pass.
3. **Grades.** Each grade against SCE1's key; a vendor's statement never carries more than vendor-reported; an abstract-only reading is said to be one.
4. **Vendors.** No real vendor assessed, ranked, compared, recommended or mapped; each real vendor named only as writer rule 5 allows; every list complete and alike; no private company's unpublished figure; no fictional vendor tied to a real one.
5. **The case.** Every case fact against `case-facts.md`; labelled fictional; never used as evidence.
6. **Scope and tone.** No AI features, no interview framing, no method teaching, nothing about the Project's owner or his employer; the preamble's tone rules; terms defined on first use.
7. **Deliverable and figures.** The deliverable against the brief's wording, built in full and then as a template; "On a site visit" and "Self-check" present and agreeing with the body; every figure's labels and numbers traced to the text.
It also runs the unit against Part 8 line by line and reads it as the reader in the preamble would. Before any fix, `python3 kit/thin.py save-report --row W-SCE14 --report out/report.md --model <the model it stated> --chat <this session's link> --date <today>` saves the report as `audits/supply-chain-execution/fresh-check-SCE14.md`; `python3 kit/thin.py assemble out/unit.md out/unsure.md "<the line it prints>"` gives the unit its unsure list. A `rewrite` verdict sends the unit to a new writer from the same brief, once; a second stops the row with a note.

**5. Apply.** A fresh applier agent (staged with `thin.py stage applier --row W-SCE14 <folder> --unit <unit> --report <report>`) verifies each finding against the unit and the cited source and writes `out/apply.json` (`apply` or `hold`, with the reason); `python3 kit/thin.py apply <unit> fixes.json out/apply.json` applies it as exact replacements, the unit left unchanged if one fails, the applier re-run on the failures once. Every `hold` is recorded with its reason; a held error or should-fix is a flag. A figure that fails is fixed and re-checked by a new agent, as many times as it takes; figures are never dropped. Restarts: when the harness says it is resuming an agent, let it and say so in the record; if an agent is lost, re-run it from the start once; if lost again, start a fresh agent on the same packet that resumes from the lost run's working files. Look for agent transcripts (`.jsonl` under `/root/.claude/` or `/tmp/`); where they exist, check by script that each agent used no tool it was denied and read nothing outside its folder, and take its `"model"` field; where there are none, say so, and the model is the one the agent stated.

**6. Mistakes in filed units of this course** are corrected where they sit, at step 7b. The course has no source units.

**7. Syllabus flags.** Append every Contradiction and Need to `courses/supply-chain-execution/notes/syllabus-flags.md` (next number, date, raised by SCE14, the units affected, the flag, the proposed change, status open); set to `carried` each flag this unit was given and dealt with. The writer's Case facts reach the table "Added by units" of `case-facts.md` through the filer at step 8 (`case_lines` in `records.json`, one row each, inserted by `thin.py insert`); the chat does not open the notes. Change no brief: `briefs.md` and the units in `course.toml` are edited only by a syllabus review that Pedro confirms. If a flag means the next unit cannot be written as briefed, say so in the first line of the reply and write a syllabus-review prompt in place of the next unit's.

**7b. Proposals now** (`docs/public-courses.md` §3 rule 12): every correction to a filed unit or a record, and every kit fix the work needs, is made in this chat before the build, under that rule's limits, each change to a unit checked by a fresh Agent on model opus first. Only a brief change and a task for Pedro stay *proposed*.

**8. File** per `skills/file-unit`, by a fresh filer agent and a script: front matter (model: the model the writer stated) on `courses/supply-chain-execution/SCE14-<slug>.md`; the filer, staged with `python3 kit/thin.py stage filer --row W-SCE14 <folder> --unit <unit> --notes <notes> --unsure <unsure> --report <report>`, writes `out/records.json` (the reading-page intro, saying what the unit is, how it was made and its known gaps, among them every reference not opened and every abstract-only claim; `STATUS`, `NEXT_UNIT`, `VERSION`, the hub `CHANGELOG` line, the case facts as `case_lines`, the flag rows, the `docs/courses-index.md` edits with a staleness row if search is on, both changelog entries, the `Object:` line); `python3 kit/thin.py insert out/records.json` inserts it and restores everything if it fails. Then `python3 kit/index_check.py` until it matches and `python3 kit/private_terms.py` prints OK.

**9. Build and verify.** `cd kit && python3 build.py` (read its last line), `python3 verify.py` (ALL CHECKS PASSED), then `python3 verify.py --shots <dir> --page read-sce14` (check the id it prints) in the background with a one-hour timeout; look at the page's shots at phone and desktop, dark and light, and at every figure's frames. A layout fault is fixed in `design/theme.css` (then `cp design/theme.css kit/shell.css`) or `kit/viz.py`, never in a page.

**10. Publish Academy once.** Before the build you publish, move the row to `docs/CHANGELOG.md`, move its entry in `docs/site-log.md` from Next to Done and save the next prompt in its row (step 11), so the published Backlog and Changelog pages are current. **Land before you publish** (`docs/method.md` §10): commit everything above, then `python3 kit/land.py land <this row> --expected <file>`; publish only after it exits 0. Right before the publish, `git fetch` and require `git rev-parse HEAD` to equal `git rev-parse origin/main` (if main has moved, `git pull --ff-only` first), rebuild that HEAD (`cd kit && python3 build.py`) and publish that build, never one built before landing. Read the artifact https://claude.ai/artifact/WwmQpUSqAUJvrcmLczppEH first and compare the live page with the build you started from (`get_artifact` for the live version id; `list_files` for every file's sha256 against `sha256sum` of the start build's files, the `kit/academy/` of the commit you cloned, saved outside the repo before any build). Send only the files that differ from the live artifact's (their sha256 in `list_files`), found by script, and the shell inside the wrapper the live page carries (its first line and its closing `</body></html>`, taken from the saved live shell by script into a copy outside the repo), with `slug`, `base_version` and mode patch; read the sent files back and compare their sha256 with the build; never a new artifact, never forced. Then `./deploy.sh` once: attach `apcanario/academy` with add_repo (if refused, carry on and ask Pedro once at the end), clone it once, set `ACADEMY_DIR` to the clone, and confirm with `gh run` that the deployment succeeded. The publish and deploy identifiers go into the records after the publish, committed and landed again with `kit/land.py`, without republishing.

**11. Close** per `docs/method.md` §8, steps 1 to 5: the row moved to `docs/CHANGELOG.md` with the date, the models and a one-line result; its entry in `docs/site-log.md` moved from Next to Done ("SCE14, Implementers and administrators, written"; "course SCE complete" on the last unit); corrections left open go to the Corrections section, proposals to Carried proposals with their ⚠ flags; the Project copies `claude/BACKLOG-chats.md` and `claude/CHANGELOG-chats.md` rewritten, and the course's three data mirrors (`claude/supply-chain-execution-course.toml`, `claude/supply-chain-execution-briefs.md`, `claude/supply-chain-execution-syllabus-flags.md`) refreshed when this row changed them, but only once they exist: S-SCE0 did not add them while sport-psychology's were in the Project (`docs/method.md` §8 step 4); the Project holds the data mirrors only of the course being written, and the row that files that course's last unit deletes them, so this course's are added by the row that files the last unit of whichever course is being written when they are due; a row of this course that finds them absent after that adds all three from their repo files (`project_write` with `local_path`); while sport-psychology's are still in the Project, none is written; `python3 kit/next_prompt.py --save` saves the prompt of W-SCE15 (unit SCE15).
